### NEM — G-1 evidence, 5 November 2026

Source owner for [nem shot references](nem-storyboard.md). G-1 draft: the analyst's review of electricity mechanics, investment meaning and Chinese is pending.

Scope: the storyboard step only. Researcher: Claude, 5 November 2026. A **confirmed** result means the narrow proposition has a primary locator that was opened and read on the check date; its editorial state is `sourced`, not `reviewed`. A **candidate** has not been checked against a primary source and cannot carry a Fair or Strong marker on its own. No source here establishes any project's award terms, floor, connection agreement or realised revenue. New IDs continue the NEM series after SRC-NEM-02, which the [WA contrast section](#australia--nem-for-the-wa-contrast) holds.

#### Live anchor and repository provenance

| ID | Inspected reference | Result and limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| REPO-NEM-LIVE | `main` at `e7eda6f18dfe7c2b49ad2f33856b646906aa010c`: [NEM view](../../view/markets/nem.json), [briefing model](../../src/briefing/model.js), [experience adapter](../../src/experience/model.js), [project facts](../../src/experience/projectFacts.js), [NEM comparison](../../src/markets/nem/comparison.js), [NEM data](../../src/markets/nem/data.js), [NEM sources](../../src/markets/nem/sources.js), [dataset catalogue](../../src/governance/datasets.js) | Briefing evaluated read-only for 5 November 2026 (`HOME_BRIEFING.on`). `infraFund`: call `opportunistic` (Situational / 择机投入), tier `situational`, `nem-ltda-4h`, route `ltda`, 4 h, operating, evidence `fair`, next trigger `nem-successor-scheme-first-round` (2027-12). Card at check: all-equity hold 7.4%, levered 7.8%, target 10.6% (not met), secured 60.5% for 15 years; capex AUD 1.33m/MW; operating ceiling AUD 1.08m/MW at 10.1%. Design grid: the award route is priced only at 4 h (+$36k/MW, the only design that clears the yields); merchant best at 6 h, every merchant cell negative; toll priced only at 4 h, negative. The model carries no capacity line (`contracted: 0`) and models the award in the floor-binding regime as 0.9 × an estimated floor plus 10% of market revenue. No NEM dataset is registered. Other lenses: `strategic` and `developer` Build position, greenfield; `foreignFirstTimer` Not accessible |

Only links, locators and paraphrases are stored; no report, table or graphic is vendored. Text records are `public-link-only`, an access boundary rather than a permission to redistribute. #### Historical nodes — events separate from interpretation

Six nodes. Dates are authoring metadata; an explicit "checked today" boundary precedes the outlook.

| Node / shots | Dated event / source | Constraint → choice → inheritance → present effect (editorial reading) | Limit / disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H-NEM-01 / nem-01, nem-02 | Formal development from 1991; NEM commenced December 1998 (SRC-NEM-14 p. 30; SRC-NEM-03) | Separate state systems → one energy-only wholesale market → regional five-minute prices → a battery earns the spot price, not a capacity payment | Events `sourced`; reading `proposed` |
| H-NEM-02 / nem-02, nem-03 | South Australia's black system, 28 September 2016 (SRC-NEM-18) | A state-wide loss of supply → tighter security and fast frequency control → fast-service markets → the first batteries' revenue | Event `sourced`; the battery link is CL-NEM-19, candidate |
| H-NEM-03 / nem-02, nem-03 | Five-minute settlement, rule 28 November 2017, starting 2021 (SRC-NEM-16) | Half-hour settlement blunted fast response → five-minute settlement → paid by the five minutes → a battery is paid for the minutes it responds | Rule and AEMC's battery statement `sourced`; start day not read |
| H-NEM-04 / nem-02 | Integrating energy storage rule, 2 December 2021 (SRC-NEM-17) | Storage registered twice → one category for storage and hybrids → the Integrated Resource Provider → storage as a participant in its own right | `sourced`; commencement not read |
| H-NEM-05 / nem-02, nem-04 | CIS pilots from 2023, expansion 23 November 2023 and 29 July 2025, tenders to 2027 (SRC-NEM-10, SRC-NEM-11) | Not enough firmed capacity before coal closes → government underwriting by tender → awards held by a minority of projects → the only long secured battery revenue | Events `sourced`; the motive is the scheme's stated purpose |
| H-NEM-06 / nem-02, nem-12 (dashed) | Review final report 16 December 2025; consultation closed 13 October 2026; ministers to consider in December 2026; Tender 10 results indicatively December 2026; first successor round indicatively late 2027 (view trigger) (SRC-NEM-09/10/14/15) | Outlook: the energy-only market kept; a successor proposed, not decided | Future items dashed; nothing drawn as completed |

| GAP-NEM-CIS | Tender rounds and the register's dispatchable counts are not stored as records | Build step: analyst-entered rows from SRC-NEM-11 and SRC-NEM-13; no project names |

| GAP-NEM-FAST | No primary record located of how the first NEM batteries earned their revenue after 2016 | Locate AEMO's or the AEMC's account (CL-NEM-19) |
| GAP-NEM-TOLL | No Australian battery toll price is public; the platform's coverage gap `contractPriceGap` is an internal assessment, not a source | Keep as a candidate (CL-NEM-18); the story shows the toll as a route only |

#### Conflicts and findings for the analyst

| ID | Finding | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CF-NEM-01 | The next action says "pay no more than build cost". The card's operating ceiling, the highest price that meets the 10.1% operating target, is AUD 1.08m/MW, below the AUD 1.33m/MW build capex; at build cost the all-equity hold return (7.4%) is below the 10.6% target | Story binds the action and the ceiling and types neither. Analyst: confirm or revise the action wording (G-1 item 3) |
| CF-NEM-02 | The play buys "an operating asset with an executed tender award". On the register at 23 October 2026, none of the 17 battery CISAs is operating; across the whole portfolio one project (a solar farm) is in operation, and most target commercial operation in 2028–29 (SRC-NEM-12, SRC-NEM-13) | Story states the dated count in `nem-11`. Analyst: whether the play's timing or wording should reflect it; GAP-NEM-OPERATING (G-1 item 4) |
| CF-NEM-03 | The thesis types a fleet that "neared 8,000 MW" and a spread "at a record low". The 7,960 MW figure is a third-party index's (June 2026, `src/markets/nem/data.js`); AEMO reports more than 9,000 MW at the end of June and more than 10 GW / 23 GWh at the end of September 2026, including commissioning. AEMO's lowest quarter in its nine-quarter window is the June quarter of 2026 ($51/MWh), but AEMO does not call it a record | Story copies neither. Analyst: refresh the thesis wording (G-1 item 5); no view edit here |
| CF-NEM-04 | The revenue-certainty grade reason types the 12 October design grid's secured share; the card shows the investment layer's 60.5% (`row.econ.secured`), and the briefing's design value is 64.3% (`legacyEcon.secured`) | Story binds the card. Analyst: update the reason or confirm its basis (G-1 item 6) |
| CF-NEM-05 | Awards are bid as fixed nominal amounts or a fixed nominal schedule (SRC-NEM-08, Table 5); the platform indexes contracted lines at 2.5% under the 29 October financing convention (`securedIndexation: 'indexed'`) | Convention settled; story rule: never say an award rises with inflation (G-1 item 7) |
| CF-NEM-06 | `src/markets/nem/sources.js`: `nelson` (dcceew.gov.au/energy/markets/national-electricity-market-review) and `mlf` (…/data-nem/network-data/marginal-loss-factors) return 404; `contractPriceGap` says the review recommends that the successor publish its clearing prices, which the final report does not say (SRC-NEM-14, pp. 189–190: forward price curves and forecasts) | Separate data change, not made here (G-1 item 8). Current pages: SRC-NEM-15 and SRC-NEM-19 |
| CF-NEM-07 | The watched trigger is the successor's first round, indicatively late 2027. Two dated steps come first: Energy Ministers' consideration of the draft law in December 2026 (SRC-NEM-15) and Tender 10's results, indicatively December 2026 (SRC-NEM-10). The review itself recommended commencement by FY2027 with a pilot in late 2026 (SRC-NEM-14, note 322) | Story shows both as context in `nem-12`. Analyst: context only, or a trigger (G-1 item 9) |
| CF-NEM-08 | The design grid prices the award route only at 4 h, so `byRoute.ltda.best` is 4 by construction; the four-hour choice rests on the tenders' four-hour-equivalent unit (SRC-NEM-09) | Story says "the size the tenders buy", not "best of several award designs" |

#### Access log, 5 November 2026

AEMO's site returned 403 to automated fetches; AEMC returned 403; DCCEEW and energy.gov.au timed out. All were read in a browser. AEMO and ASL PDFs and the QED workbook were read in the page from the publisher's own domain; nothing was saved to the repository. Navigating directly to one AEMO PDF triggered a browser download of the NEM fact sheet to the user's Downloads folder; it was read in the page instead and is not stored in the repository.

Source owner for the [Japan shot references](japan-storyboard.md). **Draft for G-1; nothing here is signed off.** One national layer serves all three allocation markets; regional records are keyed by area.

Sources marked **opened** were read on **5 November 2026** at the publisher's URL. Downloaded PDFs were read as extracted text, and one table was read as a rendered page image. Nothing downloaded is stored in the repository. Sources marked **repository-cited** are registered in `src/markets/jp/sources.js` or `src/markets/jp/gridMapData.js` with an earlier check date and were not reopened today; they must be reopened before G-2. Search summaries and law-firm notes are discovery leads only. No licence-restricted report is used, and no award holder, developer or project is named.

#### Historical nodes — events separate from interpretation

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H-JP-01 | Before April 2016 | Regulated cost-of-service tariffs (SRC-JP-01 p. 4) | Large plant needs long payback → regulated recovery → areas built for themselves → each area its own market | Event via OCCTO's account |
| H-JP-02 | 11 Mar 2011; outages from 14 Mar 2011 | Rotating planned outages in TEPCO's area (SRC-JP-06) | Eastern supply lost → rotate outages → adequacy a national issue → reform | Event sourced; supply loss and converter limit candidate; causal link proposed (CL-JP-18) |
| H-JP-03 | Apr 2015, Apr 2016, Apr 2020 | OCCTO, full retail liberalisation, legal unbundling (SRC-JP-05) | Regional monopolies → reform → short-term price setting → no guaranteed recovery | Events sourced |
| H-JP-04 | FY2020; FY2023 | Capacity market; long-term auction (SRC-JP-01 pp. 4–5) | Missing long-term predictability → multi-year award for new decarbonised capacity → award holders → the asset the call buys | Events and OCCTO's rationale sourced |
| H-JP-05 | Mar 2022 | Supply tightness in eastern Japan (SRC-JP-01 p. 4) | Thermal retirements → keep adequacy on the agenda, add gas-fired new build → yearly rounds | Event via OCCTO's account |
| H-JP-06 | Bids 27 Jan–3 Feb 2027 | Round 4: capped lithium-ion pool, certified cells first (SRC-JP-01 pp. 9–10, 35–36) | Supply-chain security → certified-first order → fewer new awards → the watched trigger | Official plan; drawn dashed |
| H-JP-TK-01 | From 1 Aug 2026 | Per-applicant cap on concurrent connection studies (SRC-JP-13) | Queue pressure → ration studies → slower new connections → a connection already held has value | Event repository-cited |
| H-JP-KY-01 | Jul 2012; 25 Sep 2014; 13 Oct 2018 | FIT, paused replies to connection applications, first mainland output control (SRC-JP-08) | Fast solar build → curtail → surplus at noon → charging windows | Events sourced |
| H-JP-KY-02 | 1 Apr 2026 | Transfer-trip system in service (SRC-JP-10) | Export limit → raise transfer over Kanmon → less curtailment expected → less surplus to charge from | Event sourced; effect is the operator's expectation |
| H-JP-HK-01 | 6 Sep 2018; link due Feb–Mar 2019 | Whole-area blackout; new Hokkaido–Honshu link (SRC-JP-07) | Small islanded system → blackout → stronger link → export still thin | Events sourced |
| H-JP-HK-02 | 10 Apr 2026 | Charging-control connection list extended (SRC-JP-11) | Slow reinforcement → connect early with charging control → non-firm charging → floor risk | Event sourced |

#### Calculation records

| ID | Question | Inputs and method | Meaning and limits | Review |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CALC-JP-FADE | Does the play's design hold the floor in the award's last year? | `termCheck` in `src/comparison/designFit.js`: year-term hours = installed hours × (1 − fade)^(term − 1); live inputs 9 h, 2 %/yr, 20 years → 6.13 h against 6 h; 6 ÷ 0.98¹⁹ = 8.81 h at commissioning would just hold it | Teaching reference for jp-09; the fade is the model's seed under the 12 Oct augmentation ruling; a holder's warranted curve replaces it in diligence | Repository arithmetic; floor ruling of 16 Oct |
| CALC-JP-PRICE | At what price does an operating holder clear the fund's target, against build cost? | No new calculation: the acquisition screen's reference cases per area (`pricePerMW`, `targetReturn`, keys cautious/base/favourable) and the play design's build cost per MW (BUILD-JP-02); discount = build cost − base price, at render | JPY per MW at acquisition; not a bid, not an observed price; the design value (USD per MW at the platform's yields) is a separate label | Proposed for G-1 |

| CF-JP-01 | `src/markets/jp/national.js` describes Round 3 as "6h receives partial credit, 8h+ full credit", while the floor ruling and SRC-JP-01 p. 13 state a six-hour continuous minimum | Both can hold: one concerns the expected-capacity factor, the other eligibility. The story uses p. 13's wording only; the credit statement needs its own locator before any page uses it |
| CF-JP-02 | Round 4 lets a battery elect an application period of 20 to 40 years (SRC-JP-01 pp. 10, 68); the plays, `economics.ltda.years` and the floor ruling assume twenty | Closed 5 Nov 2026: the analyst kept twenty years (storyboard §12). The story says "twenty years in principle"; no model or view change |
| CF-JP-03 | The dashboard's national timeline calls Kyushu's 2018 curtailment "the first sustained renewable curtailment in mainland Japan"; Kyushu Electric's page says "first on the Kyushu mainland" | Story uses the source's wording; the dashboard line is outside this task |
| CF-JP-04 | Verified output-control days cover different windows: Kyushu Jan–Mar 2026; Tokyo and Hokkaido Mar–May 2026. `jp-zones.json` calls Hokkaido "the highest curtailment"; the v1.3 card calls Kyushu "deepest" | No cross-region ranking on the page until a matched-window series exists |
| CF-JP-05 | `hokkaidoHonshu` in `sources.js` points at OCCTO's home page, while `gridMapData.js` cites HEPCO's expansion summary for the same 900 → 1,200 MW | Story binds to SRC-JP-15 (HEPCO); the weak locator is outside this task |
| CF-JP-06 | The thesis, `despite` and trigger label carry typed figures, including a trade-press range for award prices | Never copied; the trigger binds by date only |
| CF-JP-07 | The v1.3 cards propose three different signature charts; this script proposes one parameterised chart | Closed 5 Nov 2026: the analyst chose one shared chart (storyboard §12) |
| CF-JP-08 | The next action asks to list each area's award holders, but SRC-JP-02 has no area column | No holder named or counted on the page; GAP-JP-HOLDERS |
| CF-JP-09 | Verdict dates differ: Tokyo 2026-10-31; Kyushu and Hokkaido 2026-10-16 | Each region's `reviewedAgainst` records its own tuple; one review date for the script |

#### Access log, 5 November 2026

OCCTO's Round 4 guidelines, Round 3 award list and 2018 blackout report were downloaded through the fetch tool and read as extracted text outside the repository; page 10's table was read from a rendered image. METI's 006_s02_00 returned HTTP 403, as on 31 October. A TEPCO 2011 press release also returned 403, so SRC-JP-06 is used instead. Web-search summaries located SRC-JP-05 to SRC-JP-08 and were not used as evidence.

#### Discovery leads not used as sources

Search summaries on the 2018 blackout, the reform steps, Kyushu's first curtailment and the frequency converters, including the trade and manufacturer items they cited on the converter expansion; and the law-firm notes on Round 3 already cited in `docs/japan-ltda-floor-plan.md`. None supports a claim above.
